row] for row in elevation_cm]\n        self.ny, self.nx = len(self.h), len(self.h[0])\n        if self.ny < 2 or self.nx < 2 or any(len(row) != self.nx for row in self.h):\n            raise 2, max(0, int(fy)))\n        tx, ty = fx - i, fy - j\n        low, high = self.h[j], self.h[j + 1]\n        h00, h10, h01, h11 = low[i], low[i + 1], high[i], high[i + 1]\n        height = (h00 * (1 - On a bilinear patch that is at a corner.\"\"\"\n        low, high, cell = self.h[j], self.h[j + 1], self.cell\n        h00, h10, h01, h11 = low[i], low[i + 1], high[i], high[i + 1]\n        south, north = (h10 - h00) / cell, (h11 - h01) / cell\n        west, east = (h01 - h00) / cell, (h11 - h10) / cell\n        return max(math.hypot(south, west), math.hypot(south, east),\n                   math.hypot(north, west), math.hypot(north, east))\n\n\ndef drive(surface: Surface, physics: Dict[str, Any], x: float, y: float, heading_deg: float, metres: float,\n          slip_base: Dict[str, float], noise: float = 0.0, battery: Optional[float] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"One drive command: `metres` of wheel travel at `heading_deg` from (x, y).\n\n    Per step of `segment_m` wheel metres, with the ground sampled where the step starts:\n      grade  = slope along the heading (rise over run), cross = slope to the left of it\n      slip   = clip(slip_base[terrain] + slip_grade_gain[terrain] * max(0, grade) + noise, 0, slip_max)\n      the rover moves segment * (1 - slip) along the heading and\n      segment * side_slip_gain[terrain] * cross to the right (downhill across the heading)\n      energy = segment * drive_cost_per_wheel_m[terrain] + climb_cost_per_m * max(0, height gained)\n    The step is refused, and the drive ends, if it would leave the map (`stop` = \"map_boundary\")\n    or end on ground steeper than `max_tilt` (`stop` = \"tilt\"). With a `battery`, the drive also\n    ends when the battery is spent (`stop` = \"battery\").\n\n    Returns the end position, wheel and ground metres, the energy used and `stop` (None if the\n    whole command ran).\n    \"\"\"\n    radians = math.radians(heading_deg)\n    ux, uy = math.cos(radians), math.sin(radians)\n    segment_full = physics[\"segment_m\"]\n    gain, side, cost = physics[\"slip_grade_gain\"], physics[\"side_slip_gain\"], physics[\"drive_cost_per_wheel_m\"]\n    climb, slip_max, max_tilt = physics[\"climb_cost_per_m\"], physics[\"slip_max\"], physics[\"max_tilt\"]\n    width, height = surface.width, surface.height\n    remaining = float(metres)\n    wheel = ground = energy = 0.0\n    stop = None\n    here, gx, gy, code = surface.sample(x, y)\n    while remaining > 1e-9:\n        segment = min(segment_full, remaining)\n        name = TERRAIN_CODES[code]\n        grade = gx * ux + gy * uy\n        cross = -gx * uy + gy * ux\n        slip = slip_base[name] + gain[name] * (grade if grade > 0 else 0.0) + noise\n        slip = 0.0 if slip < 0 else (slip_max if slip > slip_max else slip)\n        forward = segment * (1.0 - slip)\n        sideways = segment * side[name] * cross\n        next_x = x + ux * forward + uy * sideways\n        next_y = y + uy * forward - ux * sideways\n        if not (0.0 <= next_x <= width and 0.0 <= next_y <= height):\n            stop = \"map_boundary\"\n            break\n        there, next_gx, next_gy, next_code = surface.sample(next_x, next_y)\n        if math.hypot(next_gx, next_gy) > max_tilt:\n            stop = \"tilt\"\n            break\n        spent = segment * cost[name] + climb * (there - here if there > here else 0.0)\n        if battery is not None and spent >= battery - energy:\n            energy = battery\n            wheel += segment\n            ground += math.hypot(next_x - x, next_y - y)\n            x, y = next_x, next_y\n            stop = \"battery\"\n            break\n        energy += spent\n        wheel += segment\n        ground += math.hypot(next_x - x, next_y - y)\n        remaining -= segment\n        x, y, here, gx, gy, code = next_x, next_y, there, next_gx, next_gy, next_code\n    return {\"x\": x, \"y\": y, \"wheel\": wheel, \"ground\": ground, \"energy\": energy, \"stop\": stop}\n\"\"\"Play a Lightlag MCP server from ordinary, synchronous Python.\n\n`connect(target)` yields an object with the five public methods, each making a\nreal MCP tool call. Scripted controllers can therefore run through the same\nservice a model would use:\n\n    from lightlag_controllers import REFERENCE, drive, make\n    from lightlag_mcp.client import connect\n\n    with connect(\"http://127.0.0.1:8000/mcp\") as tools:\n        drive(tools, make(REFERENCE))\n\n`target` is a URL (Streamable HTTP), `StdioServerParameters`, or a server object\nfor in-process tests. Requires the `mcp` package, version 2.x.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport asyncio\nimport concurrent.futures\nimport contextlib\nimport json\nimport threading\nfrom typing import Any, Dict, Iterator, List\n\nfrom mcp import Client\n\n\nclass RemoteToolError(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"The server rejected a call. A ValueError, like the simulator's own rejections.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass McpTools:\n    \"\"\"The five public methods, each a blocking MCP tool call.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, client: Client, loop: asyncio.AbstractEventLoop) -> None:\n        self._client = client\n        self._loop = loop\n\n    def _call(self, tool: str, arguments: Dict[str, Any]) -> Any:\n        future = asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(self._client.call_tool(tool, arguments), self._loop)\n        result = future.result()\n        text = \"\".join(getattr(block, \"text\", \"\") for block in result.content)\n        if result.is_error:\n            raise RemoteToolError(text)\n        return json.loads(text)\n\n    def rules(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n        return self._call(\"rules\", {})\n\n    def map(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n        return self._call(\"map\", {})\n\n    def telemetry_log(self) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n        return self._call(\"telemetry_log\", {})\n\n    def uplink(self, commands: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n        return self._call(\"uplink\", {\"commands\": commands})\n\n    def advance(self, ticks: int = 1) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n        return self._call(\"advance\", {\"ticks\": ticks})\n\n    def tool_names(self) -> List[str]:\n        future = asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(self._client.list_tools(), self._loop)\n        return [tool.name for tool in future.result().tools]\n\n\n@contextlib.contextmanager\ndef connect(target: Any) -> Iterator[McpTools]:\n    \"\"\"Open an MCP session on a background event loop and yield blocking tools.\"\"\"\n    loop = asyncio.new_event_loop()\n    thread = threading.Thread(target=loop.run_forever, name=\"lightlag-mcp-client\", daemon=True)\n    thread.start()\n    ready: \"concurrent.futures.Future[Client]\" = concurrent.futures.Future()\n    stop_holder: List[asyncio.Event] = []\n\n    async def session() -> None:\n        # output goes to ./autopilot.log\n    python3 /app/detach.py --log second.log python3 autopilot.py\n\nPrints the process id and returns at once. Stop the program with `kill <pid>`.\n\nWhy this exists. An agent's shell tool runs one command at a time under a time limit. Such a\ntool typically waits until everything the command started has let go of its output, and when\nthe limit is reached it stops the whole process group. A program started with `&` or `nohup`\nis still in that group, and in a form like `cd /app && nohup python3 autopilot.py > log 2>&1 &`\nthe shell that waits for it still holds the tool's output. The call then hangs until the limit\nand the program is killed with it, in the middle of a campaign that does not wait.\n\nA program started here has its own session and no link to the caller's input or output: it\nreads from nowhere and writes to the log file, unbuffered so the log is current.
